3.29
) from (
3.33
).

Now, suppose the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform. In this case, (
5.17
) holds. Using (
3.11
), (
3.32
), and Hölder’s and Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}+\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

proving (
3.30
). Finally, writing

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right)

\displaystyle=\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right|^{2}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u})+(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right|^{2}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right|^{2})\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}+2\left[\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\cdot(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u})\right]\boldsymbol{u}_{h}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left[(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u})\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right]\boldsymbol{u}_{h}+2\left(\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right)(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}),

then applying Hölder’s and Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\partial_{t}\left(|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right),\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}+\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}+\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}+\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}+2\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2},

thus proving (
3.31
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We will prove that the semi-discrete scheme converges at an optimal rate. For simplicity of presentation, we will assume that
\boldsymbol{u}_{0,h}=R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}(0)
is the approximation in
\mathbb{V}_{h}
of the initial data, so that
\boldsymbol{\theta}(0)=0
. In this case, subtracting the second equation in (
2.1
) from that in (
3.1
) yields

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle=-\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\kappa\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle.

Therefore, at
t=0
, noting (
3.32
) and taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\boldsymbol{\xi}(0)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(0)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}(0),\boldsymbol{\xi}(0)\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho}(0),\boldsymbol{\xi}(0)\right\rangle-\kappa\left\langle|R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}(0)|^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}(0)-\big(\boldsymbol{\rho}(0)\cdot(R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}(0)+\boldsymbol{u}(0))\big)\boldsymbol{u}(0),\boldsymbol{\xi}(0)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}(0)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}(0)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(0)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

for any
\epsilon>0
by Young’s inequality. Rearranging the above, then applying (
2.8
) and (
2.8
), we obtain

(3.34)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(0)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\lesssim h^{r+1}.

Next, we prove some bounds for
\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)
and
\boldsymbol{\xi}(t)
for
t\in(0,T)
. In particular, (
3.35
) shows a superconvergence estimate for
\boldsymbol{\theta}
, which implies the corresponding estimate (
3.36
) in the
\mathbb{L}^{\infty}
norm under an additional assumption of global quasi-uniformity of the triangulation.

Proposition 3.8
.

For any
t\in(0,T)
,

(3.35)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{2(r+1)}.

Moreover, if the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is globally quasi-uniform then

(3.36)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{2(r+1)},

(3.37)

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{2r}.

The constant
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
|\mathscr{D}|
,
T
, and
K_{0}
(as defined in (
2.2
)), but is independent of
h
.

Proof.

Subtracting (
2.1
) from (
3.1
), using (
3.16
), (
3.17
) (and noting the definition of Ritz projection), we obtain for all
\boldsymbol{\chi}
,
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

(3.38)

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}+\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi},\nabla\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

and

(3.39)

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle=-\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\kappa\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle

Taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\boldsymbol{\theta}
in (
3.38
), we have

(3.40)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi},\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle.

Taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{r}\boldsymbol{\theta}
in (
3.39
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle=

\displaystyle-\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle

(3.41)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle.

Next, taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{e}\boldsymbol{\xi}
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

(3.42)

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle.

Substituting (
3.41
) into (
3.40
), then adding the result to (
3.42
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\beta\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle.

Applying Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, (
3.20
), (
3.26
), and (
3.27
) to the above equation yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\right)+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\beta\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\big|+\kappa\lambda_{e}\big|\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\lambda_{r}\big|\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+h^{2(r+1)}+\big(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(3.43)

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\big(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

for any
\epsilon>0
, where the constant is independent of
h
,
t
, and
T
. Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small and integrating over
(0,t)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}(0)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+h^{2(r+1)}+\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

Note that by Proposition
3.1
and (
2.10
),

\int_{0}^{t}1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim 1+\int_{0}^{t}\big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}+\left\|{R_{h}\boldsymbol{H}_{h}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}\big)\mathrm{d}s\lesssim 1,

and so Gronwall’s inequality applied to (
3.43
) yields

(3.44)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq Ch^{2(r+1)}.

Next, differentiating (
3.39
) with respect to
t
, then taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{e}\boldsymbol{\xi}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}+\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

(3.45)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\partial_{t}(|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle.

Taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\boldsymbol{\xi}
and rearranging the terms, we have

\displaystyle\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}+\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

(3.46)

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle.

Adding (
3.45
) and (
3.46
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

(3.47)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\partial_{t}(|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle.

Furthermore, taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}
in (
3.38
) gives

(3.48)

\displaystyle\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\eta},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi},\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle.

Taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{r}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}
in (
3.39
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\eta},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle

(3.49)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}),\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle.

Substituting (
3.49
) into (
3.48
), then adding the result to (
3.47
) give

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\partial_{t}(|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}),\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle.

Applying Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities to bound the terms on the second line, then using (
3.22
), (
3.28
) and (
3.29
) for the remaining terms yield

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\big|+\gamma\big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\lambda_{e}\big|\left\langle\partial_{t}(|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle\big|+\kappa\lambda_{r}\big|\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\big|+\beta\lambda_{r}\big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}),\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+h^{2(r+1)}+\big(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+h^{2(r+1)}+\big(1+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\big(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}

for any
\epsilon>0
, where the constant is independent of
h
,
t
, and
T
. Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, then integrating over
(0,t)
and using (
3.44
) (and noting (
3.34
)), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{B}(s)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s,

where
\mathcal{B}(s):=1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}
. Note that by Proposition
3.1
, Proposition
3.2
, and inequality (
2.10
),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{B}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{R_{h}\boldsymbol{H}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{R_{h}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\lesssim 1.

Therefore, by Gronwall’s inequality,

(3.50)

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq Ch^{2(r+1)}.

Inequality (
3.35
) then follows from (
3.44
) and (
3.50
).

Suppose now that the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform. Taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}
in (
3.39
), rearranging the terms, then applying Young’s inequality and (
3.30
) give

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\eta},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{\rho},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle+\kappa\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle-\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used (
3.35
) shown above. Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small then yields

(3.51)

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}.

Next, we take
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}
and apply the same argument (using (
3.35
) and (
3.23
) this time) to obtain

(3.52)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}.

Inequality (
3.36
) then follows from (
3.51
), (
3.52
), and (
2.18
).

Finally, setting
\boldsymbol{\chi}=-\Delta_{h}^{2}\boldsymbol{\xi}
and applying (
2.6
) as necessary, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\partial_{t}\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta},\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

(3.53)

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H}),\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle.

Differentiating the second equation in (
3.1
) with respect to
t
, then setting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=-\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle+\left\langle\partial_{t}\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta},\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}+\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

(3.54)

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\left\langle\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle+\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

Adding (
3.53
) and (
3.54
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}+\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle+\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{H}),\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle+\kappa\left\langle\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle.

The first four terms on the right-hand side can be estimated using Young’s inequality (together with (
2.8
)) in a straightforward manner. The last two terms can be bounded using (
3.24
) and (
3.31
). We then have for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2r}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}

Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, integrating both sides with respect to
t
(and noting (
3.35
)), we obtain (
3.37
). This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

We are now ready to state the main theorem of this section on the order of convergence for the semi-discrete scheme (
3.1
).

Theorem 3.9
.

Let
(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{H})
be the solution of (
2.1
) as described in Section
2.2
, and let
(\boldsymbol{u}_{h},\boldsymbol{H}_{h})\in\mathbb{V}_{h}\times\mathbb{V}_{h}
be the solution of (
3.1
) with initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
. Then

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}+h\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{r+1},

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}+h\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\nabla\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{r+1}.

Moreover, if the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform, then for
s=0
or
1
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{s})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{s})}

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{r+1-s},

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}-\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{r+1}\left|{\ln h}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The constant
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
|\mathscr{D}|
,
T
, and
K_{0}
(as defined in (
2.2
)), but is independent of
h
.

Proof.

The result follows from Proposition
3.8
, equations (
3.16
) and (
3.17
), estimates (
2.8
), (
2.9
), and the triangle inequality.
∎

4.
A Fully Discrete Scheme Based on the Semi-Implicit Euler Method

We propose a time-discrete scheme for the LLBar equation using the semi-implicit Euler method. First, we fix some notations. Let
k
be the time step and
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
be the approximation in
\mathbb{V}_{h}
of
\boldsymbol{u}(t)
at time
t=t_{n}:=nk
, where
n=0,1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor
. For any function
\boldsymbol{v}
, we denote
\boldsymbol{v}^{n}:=\boldsymbol{v}(t_{n})
, and define

\displaystyle\delta\boldsymbol{v}^{n+1}:=\frac{\boldsymbol{v}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{v}^{n}}{k},\quad\text{for }n=0,1,\ldots.

A fully discrete scheme can now be described as follows. We start with
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}=R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}(0)\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
for simplicity. For
t_{n}\in[0,T]
where
n\in\mathbb{N}
, given
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
, define
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
and
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}
by

(4.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle&=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\nabla\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle\\
\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle&=-\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\kappa\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle,\end{aligned}\right.

for all
\boldsymbol{\chi},\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
.

Note that under the assumptions (
2.2
), for
p\in[1,\infty]
we have

(4.2)

\displaystyle\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{p}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\frac{1}{k}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{p}}\leq\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{p}},

(4.3)

\displaystyle\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{p}}+\left\|{\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\partial_{tt}\boldsymbol{u}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{p}}\leq Ck,

where Taylor’s theorem was used in the last step of (
4.3
), and
C
depends on
\left\|{\partial_{tt}\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{p})}
.

We now show that the scheme (
4.1
) is well-posed for any time step size
k
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
and
k>0
be given. For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, given
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
, there exists unique
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
and
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}
solving (
4.1
).

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{X}:=\mathbb{V}_{h}\times\mathbb{V}_{h}
(which is a subspace of
\mathbb{H}^{1}\times\mathbb{H}^{1}
), equipped with norm
\left\|{(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})}\right\|_{\mathbb{X}}:=\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
. Define a nonlinear form
\mathcal{A}:\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}),(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\big)

\displaystyle:=\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle+k\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle+k\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{v},\nabla\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle-k\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\;\quad+\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle+\kappa\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle+\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle.

and a linear form
f:\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{R}
by

f\big((\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\big):=\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle.

Multiplying the first equation in (
4.1
) by
-k
and adding it to the second equation, we see that solving (
4.1
) is equivalent to solving

(4.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\left((\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}),(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\right)=f\big((\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\big),\quad\forall(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\in\mathbb{X}.

For each fixed
(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\in\mathbb{X}
, the map
(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\mapsto\mathcal{A}\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}),(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\big)
is a bounded linear functional. Thus, there exists a map
T:\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\ast}
defined by

(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\mapsto T((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})),\quad\text{such that}\quad\mathcal{A}\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}),(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\big)=\left\langle T\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\big),(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\right\rangle,\quad\forall(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\in\mathbb{X},

where
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
denotes the duality pairing which extends the usual
\mathbb{L}^{2}
-inner product.
We aim to use the Browder–Minty theorem to deduce the existence and uniqueness of (
4.4
). To this end, it remains to show
T
is bounded, continuous, strictly monotone, and coercive.

Boundedness
: We have by Hölder’s inequality and Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\subset\mathbb{L}^{6}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{T\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{X}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle:=\sup\left\{\left|\mathcal{A}\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}),(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\big)\right|:(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})\in\mathbb{X}\text{ and }\left\|{(\boldsymbol{\phi},\boldsymbol{\chi})}\right\|_{\mathbb{X}}\leq 1\right\}

\displaystyle\;\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}+\left(1+k\lambda_{r}+k\lambda_{e}+k\gamma\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{3}\leq C\left\|{(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})}\right\|_{\mathbb{X}}\left(1+\left\|{(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})}\right\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{2}\right).

This shows
T
maps bounded sets in
\mathbb{X}
into bounded sets in
\mathbb{X}^{\ast}
.

Continuity
: It is clear that
(\boldsymbol{u}_{n},\boldsymbol{v}_{n})\to(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})
strongly in
\mathbb{X}
implies
\left\|{T\big((\boldsymbol{u}_{n},\boldsymbol{v}_{n})\big)-T\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{X}^{\ast}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. The details are omitted.

Strict monotonicity
: Let
(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}),(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{X}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\langle T\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\big)-T\big((\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})\big),(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})-(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}),(\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})\big)-\mathcal{A}\big((\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}),(\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle=k\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}-\nabla\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\beta\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime})}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right|^{2}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right|\left|{\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}}\right|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}}\right|\left|{\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}}\right|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\geq 0,

where in the last step we used (
2.13
). Moreover, equality holds if and only if
(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})=(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})
. This shows the strict monotonicity of
T
.

Coercivity
: Let
(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\in\mathbb{X}
. We have

\displaystyle\left\langle T\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\big),(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}),(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\big)

\displaystyle=k\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}+\beta\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C_{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}-\kappa\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|^{-4}

\displaystyle\geq\min\{C_{1},C_{2}\}\left\|{(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})}\right\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{2}-\kappa\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|^{-4},

where
C_{1}:=\min\{k\lambda_{r},k\lambda_{e}\}
and
C_{2}:=\min\{1,\kappa\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|^{-4}\}
are both positive constants, and we also used the inequality

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}\geq\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|^{-4}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-1\right),

which follows from Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\left\langle T\big((\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\big),(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\right\rangle}{\left\|{(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})}\right\|_{\mathbb{X}}}\to\infty\quad\text{as }\left\|{(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})}\right\|_{\mathbb{X}}\to\infty,

showing the coercivity of
T
.

The existence and uniqueness of
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
and
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}
solving (
4.1
) then follows from the Browder–Minty theorem. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Next, we show some stability results. The following proposition shows that
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
is stable in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{1})
norm, while
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}
is stable in
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{1})
norm (over an arbitrary number of iterations
n
, even if
T=\infty
). Moreover, the energy dissipation property (
4.5
) is satisfied unconditionally.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
be given and let
\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}\right)
be defined by
(
4.1
). Then for any
k>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1})\leq\mathcal{E}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}),

where
\mathcal{E}
was defined in (
1.5
). Moreover,

(4.6)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+k\lambda_{r}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\lambda_{e}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+|\mathscr{D}|\right),

where
C
depends only on
\kappa
and
\mu
.

Proof.

Setting
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}
in (
4.1
) gives

(4.7)

\displaystyle\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

while setting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
gives

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2k}\big(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)-\frac{1}{2k}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\kappa}{4k}\left(\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}-\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}-\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)-\frac{\kappa}{4k}\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\kappa k}{2}\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|\left|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\frac{\mu k}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(4.8)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\beta}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)-\frac{\beta}{2k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n})}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Substituting (
4.8
) into (
4.7
), using identities (
2.11
) and (
2.12
), and rearranging the terms yield

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)+\frac{\kappa}{4}\left(\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}-\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}-\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\beta}{2}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{4}\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\kappa k^{2}}{2}\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|\left|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\mu k^{2}}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\beta}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n})}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+k\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}=0,

which implies (
4.5
). Finally, applying similar arguments as (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) yield (
4.6
).
∎

We also derive the stability of
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{2})
norm. The following identity will be used in the proof:

(4.9)

\displaystyle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}

\displaystyle=\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\right)+\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\right)\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\right)\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
be given and let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
,
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}
be defined by (
4.1
). Then for any
k>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.10)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=1}^{n}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C,

where
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
K_{0}
, and
|\mathscr{D}|
, but is independent of
n
,
k
, and
T
.

Proof.

Taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
in (
4.1
) gives

(4.11)

\displaystyle\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle.

Subtracting the second equation in (
4.1
) at time step
n-1
from the same equation at time step
n
, then setting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{e}\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}/k
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}),\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.12)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\cdot(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n})\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle,

where we used (
2.11
) for the left-hand side, and (
4.9
) for the last inner product. Adding (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=(\lambda_{r}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e})\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}),\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\cdot(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n})\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=:S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}+S_{4}.

We will estimate each term on the right-hand side by applying Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities. Firstly, for the terms
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
,

(4.13)

\displaystyle|S_{1}|+|S_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the third term, similarly we have

(4.14)

\displaystyle|S_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used (
4.6
) and the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{4}
. Similarly for the last term,

\displaystyle|S_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

(4.15)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, upon rearranging and summing the terms, (
4.13
), (
4.14
), and (
4.15
) imply

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\sum_{m=0}^{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+1}-\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+Ck\sum_{m=0}^{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq C,

as required.
∎

The above proposition implies the stability of
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})
norm under an additional assumption that the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
be given and let
\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}\right)
be defined by (
4.1
). Then for any
k>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.16)

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C.

Moreover, if the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is (globally) quasi-uniform, then

(4.17)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\leq C.

Here, the constant
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
K_{0}
, and
|\mathscr{D}|
, but is independent of
n
,
k
, and
T
.

Proof.

Taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
and applying Young’s and Hölder’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+(\kappa^{2}\mu^{2}+\beta^{2})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{3}{8}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{6}.

Therefore, rearranging the terms, using the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{6}
(noting (
4.6
) and (
4.10
)), we infer (
4.16
).
Inequality (
4.17
) then follows from (
2.18
), completing the proof of the proposition.
∎

Furthermore, in dimensions 1 and 2 (or
d=3
with an additional quasi-uniformity assumption on the triangulation), we can derive the stability of
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{1})
.

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
be given and let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
,
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}
be defined by (
4.1
). Suppose that one of the following holds:

(1)

d=1
or
2
,

(2)

d=3
and the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform.

Then for any
k>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.18)

\displaystyle\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=1}^{n}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=1}^{n}\left\|{\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C,

where
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
K_{0}
, and
|\mathscr{D}|
, but is independent of
n
,
k
, and
T
.

Proof.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, subtracting the first equation in (
4.1
) at time step
n
from the corresponding equation at time step
n+1
, dividing by
k
, then setting
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
give

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2k}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.19)

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}\times\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle.

Applying the same operations to the second equation in (
4.1
), then successively setting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{e}\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\Lambda\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
(where
\Lambda:=\lambda_{r}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}
) yield

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1},\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}),\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.20)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\left((\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n})\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right)\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle,

and (noting the identity (
2.13
)),

(4.21)

\displaystyle\Lambda\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\Lambda\left(\left\|{\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\kappa\mu\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle+P\right).

In the last step, we denoted
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}:=\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n})
and

P:=\kappa\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{2}\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|\left|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{2}\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|\left|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\beta\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Adding (
4.19
), (
4.20
), and (
4.21
), then applying Young’s inequality, we obtain for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2k}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\Lambda\left\|{\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+P

\displaystyle\;=\Lambda\kappa\mu\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\left((\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n})\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right)\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}),\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}\times\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\;\leq C\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{8}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{8}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+C\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}.

Note that
P\geq 0
. The expression in the last step can be estimated as follows.

Case 1:
d=1
or
2
. By Young’s inequality, Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{8}
, and (
2.14
), we have

(4.22)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, multiplyting by
2k
, rearranging the terms, then summing over
m\in\{1,2,\ldots,n-1\}
, we infer that

\displaystyle\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=1}^{n-1}\left(\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)\leq\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+Ck\sum_{m=1}^{n-1}\left(1+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Note that by taking
n=0
and
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}
in the first equation of (
4.1
), we have

\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim 1.

Therefore, the discrete Gronwall inequality and (
4.6
) then imply (
4.18
).

Case 2:
d=3
and the triangulation is quasi-uniform
. In this case, we apply Young’s inequality, (
4.17
), and (
2.14
) instead to infer (
4.22
).
The same conclusion is then attained.
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

Suppose that the hypotheses of Proposition
4.5
hold. Then for any
k>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C,

where
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
K_{0}
, and
|\mathscr{D}|
, but is independent of
n
,
k
, and
T
.

Proof.

Setting
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}
in (
4.1
) gives

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}=\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C,

where in the last step we used (
4.10
) and (
4.18
).
∎

Remark 4.7
.

Proposition
4.5
and
4.6
also hold under a technical smallness assumption on the initial data similar to Proposition
3.4
. We will not elaborate them further for brevity.

To derive optimal error estimates for the fully discrete scheme, we begin with some preparatory results. As done previously, we write

(4.24)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})

\displaystyle=\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})\big)+\big(R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})\big)=:\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n},

(4.25)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{H}(t_{n})

\displaystyle=\big(\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}-R_{h}\boldsymbol{H}(t_{n})\big)+\big(R_{h}\boldsymbol{H}(t_{n})-\boldsymbol{H}(t_{n})\big)=:\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n}.

Recall that by the definition of Ritz projection,

(4.26)

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n},\nabla\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle=\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n},\nabla\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle=0,\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}.

The following lemmas are needed to bound the nonlinear terms in the main theorem.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrary. The following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(4.27)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(4.28)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}

(4.29)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, if the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is globally quasi-uniform, then for any
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

(4.30)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(4.31)

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}\big),\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2r}+k^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Proof.

We write

(4.32)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1}\right)+\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}-k\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}.

The proof of (
4.27
) then follows by arguments similar to that in (
3.20
) (noting (
4.2
)), without assuming
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is globally quasi-uniform. Similarly, the proof of (
4.28
) follows that in (
3.22
).

Next, following (
3.19
), we write

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}+R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\times(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1})+(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}-k\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1})\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}.

The proof of (
4.29
) then follows along the line of (
3.22
) with obvious modifications.

Now, suppose the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform (and thus (
4.17
) holds in this case). Noting (
4.32
), by Hölder’s inequality and assumptions (
2.2
) on the exact solution, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+k\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{r+1}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+k,

where in the last step we used (
4.17
) and (
2.8
). Estimate (
4.30
) follows by Young’s inequality.
Similarly, noting (
2.5
), we obtain (
4.31
) following the proof of (
3.24
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrary. The following inequalities hold:

(4.33)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(4.34)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\big(1+\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}

(4.35)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Suppose now that the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is globally quasi-uniform. Then for any
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

(4.36)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

(4.37)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2r}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Proof.

First, we write

\displaystyle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}

(4.38)

\displaystyle=\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\right)+\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\right)\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}.

The proof of (
4.33
) then follows the same argument as that of (
3.26
).

Next, noting
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}
and following (
3.18
), we write

\displaystyle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}

\displaystyle=\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+2R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1})\big)\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\left|{R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\big)

(4.39)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\cdot(R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1})\big)\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}.

Applying the same argument as in the proof of (
3.27
), we then obtain (
4.34
). The proof of (
4.35
) follows along the line of (
3.28
) (by replacing
\boldsymbol{\theta}
,
\boldsymbol{\rho}
,
\boldsymbol{u}
, and
\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}
with
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}
,
\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}
,
\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}
, and
\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}
respectively).

Next, if the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is quasi-uniform, then (
4.17
) holds. As such, we can bound the
\mathbb{L}^{\infty}
norms of
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
appearing in (
4.38
) (uniformly in
n
and
h
), giving

\displaystyle\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

The estimate (
4.36
) then follows by Young’s inequality.

It remains to prove (
4.37
). Let
S
be the right-hand side of (
4.38
). Then we have

(4.40)

\displaystyle\left\langle S,\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle=\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}S,\nabla\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle.

We will proceed by estimating
\left\|{\nabla S}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
using the expression (
4.39
). By the product rule for gradient,

\displaystyle\nabla S

\displaystyle=2\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\right)+\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\left(\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\right)\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}+\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\right)\cdot\left(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\right)\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}.

Therefore, by Hölder’s inequalities and Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{6}
(and noting (
4.17
)), it is straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla S}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}+h^{r}.

Inequality (
4.37
) then follows by applying Young’s inequality and (
2.5
) to (
4.40
).
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrary.

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)

(4.41)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, if the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is globally quasi-uniform, then for any
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right),\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(4.42)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}.

Proof.
