After some tedious algebra, we can write the first component in the inner product on the left-hand side as

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle:=\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)

\displaystyle\;=\left|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\left(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)+\left[\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\big)\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\big)\right]\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\;\quad+\left[\left(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}+R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)\right]\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle\;\quad+\left[\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-2\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)\right]\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)+2\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)

(4.43)

\displaystyle=:E_{1}+E_{2}+E_{3}+E_{4}+E_{5}.

We want to obtain a bound for
\left|\left\langle E_{i},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle\right|
for
i=1,2,\cdots,5
. First, we note the frequently used inequality

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{D}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}:=\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.44)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+h^{r+1}+k,

where in the last step (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) were used.
Now, we begin to estimate the term with
E_{1}
by applying Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities (noting (
4.6
) and (
2.10
)), giving

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle E_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\mathcal{D}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}+\left\|{R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|{\mathcal{D}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\mathcal{D}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(4.45)

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Similarly, for the next term,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle E_{2},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.46)

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the term with
E_{3}
, by similar argument we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle E_{3},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\mathcal{D}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}+\left\|{\mathcal{D}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\mathcal{D}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}+R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\mathcal{D}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}+R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.47)

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

By the same argument, we also have

(4.48)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle E_{4},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

and

(4.49)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle E_{5},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, (
4.45
), (
4.46
), (
4.47
), (
4.48
), and (
4.49
) yield (
4.41
).

Next, with
E
as defined in (
4.43
), we have the identity

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\left(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)+\left[\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}\big)\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\big)\right]\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\quad+\left[\big(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\big)\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\big)\right]\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}

\displaystyle\quad+\left[\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-2\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\big)\right]\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}+2\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right).

Therefore using the assumption (
2.2
), applying Young’s inequality, (
4.44
), and Taylor’s theorem (noting that (
4.17
) can be applied by the quasi-uniformity of
\mathcal{T}_{h}
), we can follow the proof of (
3.31
) to obtain the estimate (
4.42
).
∎

We also have the following superconvergence estimates on
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}
and
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}
analogous to Proposition
3.8
, which is an essential step in the proof of the main theorem.

Proposition 4.11
.

Suppose
\boldsymbol{u}
and
\boldsymbol{H}
satisfy (
2.2
). Then for
h,k>0
and
n\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor\}
,

(4.50)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}\big).

Assume further that one of the following holds:

(1)

d=1
or
2
,

(2)

d=3
and the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform.

Then for
h,k>0
and
n\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor\}
,

(4.51)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}\big).

Finally, for
d=1
,
2
,
3
, if the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform, then

(4.52)

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}\big),

(4.53)

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2r}+k^{2}\big).

The constant
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
|\mathscr{D}|
,
T
, and
K_{0}
(as defined in (
2.2
)), but is independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
.

Proof.

Subtracting (
2.1
) from (
4.1
) at time step
n+1
, using (
4.24
), (
4.25
) (and noting the definition of Ritz projection), we obtain for all
\boldsymbol{\chi}
,
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1},\nabla\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

(4.54)

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

and

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle+\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle

(4.55)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1})\big),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle.

Taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}
in (
4.54
) and
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{r}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}
in (
4.55
), then adding the resulting equations, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1},\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.56)

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle.

Next, taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{e}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.57)

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle.

Adding (
4.56
) and (
4.57
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\beta\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle.

It remains to bound each term on the right-hand side. We apply (
4.3
), Young’s inequality, and Taylor’s theorem as necessary for the first eight terms. The last three terms can be estimated by applying Lemma
4.8
and Lemma
4.9
. Hence, we infer that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, rearranging the terms, and summing over
m\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}\big)+Ck\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where
C
is a constant depending on the coefficients of the equation and
T
(but is independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
). The discrete Gronwall inequality then yields (
4.50
).

We aim to prove (
4.51
) next. First, consider the difference of the second equation in (
4.1
) at time steps
n+1
and
n
. After dividing the result by
k
, subtracting it from the corresponding equation in (
2.1
) and setting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{e}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.58)

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1})\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle.

Next, setting
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}
gives

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.59)

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle,

while taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}
yields

\displaystyle\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.60)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1},\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle.

Furthermore, setting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{r}\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}
in (
4.55
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2k}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2k}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.61)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1})\big),\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle.

We now add (
4.58
), (
4.59
), (
4.60
), and (
4.61
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2k}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2k}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1})\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1})\big),\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.62)

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}+\cdots+I_{15}.

There are fifteen terms involving inner products on the right-hand side of (
4.62
), which we will estimate in the following. Let
\epsilon>0
be a number. Firstly, by Young’s inequality, (
2.8
), and (
4.2
),

(4.63)

\displaystyle\left|{I_{1}}\right|\lesssim\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Secondly, by Young’s inequality and Taylor’s theorem (noting the assumption on
\boldsymbol{H}
in (
2.2
)),

\displaystyle\left|{I_{2}}\right|\lesssim\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim k^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

By similar argument (noting (
2.2
)), the third term can be estimated as

\displaystyle\left|{I_{3}}\right|\lesssim\left\|{\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim k^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the fourth term, similarly by Young’s inequality, (
2.8
), (
4.2
), and (
4.3
), we have

(4.64)

\displaystyle\left|{I_{4}}\right|\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the fifth term, we use Lemma
4.10
. Noting that by assumptions (
4.23
) holds, so that

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{R_{h}\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq C,

we then obtain (noting (
4.50
))

\displaystyle\left|{I_{5}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
I_{6}
and
I_{7}
, the same argument as in (
4.64
) yields

\displaystyle\left|{I_{6}}\right|+\left|{I_{7}}\right|\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}.

Similarly, for the terms
I_{8}
and
I_{10}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{8}}\right|+\left|{I_{10}}\right|\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}.

Next, we estimate the terms
I_{9}
and
I_{11}
.
By (
4.28
) and (
4.29
), noting (
4.23
) and (
4.50
), we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{9}}\right|+\left|{I_{11}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

The term
I_{12}
can be estimated using Young’s inequality and (
4.50
) as

(4.65)

\displaystyle\left|{I_{12}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

To estimate the term
I_{13}
, note that by (
4.24
) and the triangle inequality (
4.18
),

\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq 2\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+2\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+2\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C,

where in the last step we used (
4.18
), (
2.2
), (
4.2
), and (
2.8
). Thus, applying (
4.35
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{13}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Finally, the last two terms can be estimated following (
4.64
) and (
4.65
) to obtain

(4.66)

\displaystyle|I_{14}|+|I_{15}|\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, continuing from (
4.62
), using estimates (
4.63
)–(
4.66
), choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, and rearranging the terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2k}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(4.67)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}\big)+C\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Multiplying both sides of (
4.67
) by
2k
, summing over
m\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\}
, and applying the discrete Gronwall inequality, we obtain (
4.51
).

Finally, suppose that the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform. Setting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}
in (
4.55
), we then apply Young’s inequality and (
4.37
) with
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}
to infer

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\left\langle\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1})\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used inequalities (
4.50
) and (
4.51
), (
2.8
), and Taylor’s theorem. Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small and rearranging the terms, we obtain

(4.68)

\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}.

Next, taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}
and applying similar argument (using (
4.30
) with
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}
), we obtain

(4.69)

k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}.

Inequality (
4.52
) then follows from (
4.68
), (
4.69
), and (
2.18
).

Finally, setting
\boldsymbol{\chi}=-\Delta_{h}^{2}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}
in (
4.54
) and applying (
2.6
) as necessary, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\delta\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1},\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\left(\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right),\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1},\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.70)

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{r}\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\gamma\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}),\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle.

Next, consider the difference of the second equation in (
4.1
) at time steps
n+1
and
n
. After dividing the result by
k
, subtracting it from the corresponding equation in (
2.1
) and taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=-\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2k}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\delta\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\left\langle\delta\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1},\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\left\langle\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

(4.71)

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1})\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle.

Adding (
4.70
) and (
4.71
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2k}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\left(\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right),\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1},\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{r}\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle+\gamma\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\times\boldsymbol{H}^{n+1}),\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\delta\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\left\langle\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1})\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}\right\rangle.

The first three terms on the right-hand side can be estimated using Young’s inequality (together with (
2.8
) and (
4.3
)) in a straightforward manner. The fifth and the sixth terms can also be bounded similarly. To estimate the remaining terms, we use (
4.31
) and (
4.42
).
We then have for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2k}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2r}+\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, multiplying both sides by
2k
, then using (
4.50
) and (
4.51
)), we obtain (
4.53
). This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

We can now state the main result on the rate of convergence of the scheme (
4.1
).

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{H})
be the solution of (
2.1
) as described in Section
2.2
, and let
(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n})\in\mathbb{V}_{h}\times\mathbb{V}_{h}
be the solution of (
4.1
) with initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}
. Then for
h,k>0
and
n\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+1}-\boldsymbol{H}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{2}\big),

\displaystyle k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{m+1}-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2r}+k^{2}\big).

Assume further that one of the following holds:

(1)

d=1
or
2
,

(2)

d=3
and the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform.

Then for
h,k>0
and
n\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{H}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k\big),

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+1}-\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{m+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2r}+k\big).

Finally, for
d=1
,
2
,
3
, if the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform, then

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}-\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2r}+k^{2}\big),

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m}-\boldsymbol{H}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}\left|{\ln h}\right|+k^{2}\big).

The constant
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
|\mathscr{D}|
,
T
, and
K_{0}
(as defined in (
2.2
)), but is independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
.

Proof.

The results follow from Proposition
3.8
, equations (
3.16
) and (
3.17
), estimates (
2.8
), (
2.9
), and the triangle inequality.
∎

5.
A Fully Discrete Scheme Based on the Crank–Nicolson Method

We now propose a second-order in time numerical scheme for the LLBar equation. Let
k
be the time step and
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
be the approximation in
\mathbb{V}_{h}
of
\boldsymbol{u}(t)
at time
t=t_{n}:=nk
, where
n=0,1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor
.
For any function
\boldsymbol{v}
, we denote
\boldsymbol{v}^{n}:=\boldsymbol{v}(t_{n})
. In this section, we define

\displaystyle\delta\boldsymbol{v}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}:=\frac{\boldsymbol{v}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{v}^{n}}{k},\quad\boldsymbol{v}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}:=\frac{\boldsymbol{v}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{v}^{n}}{2},\quad\text{for }n=0,1,\ldots.

Moreover, define

(5.1)

\displaystyle\delta\boldsymbol{v}^{n}:=\frac{\boldsymbol{v}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{v}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}{k},\quad\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}:=\frac{3\boldsymbol{v}^{n}-\boldsymbol{v}^{n-1}}{2},\quad\text{for }n=1,2,\ldots.

A fully discrete scheme based on the Crank–Nicolson time-stepping method can be described as follows. We start with
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}=R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}(0)\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
. For
t_{n}\in[0,T]
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, given
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
, define
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
and
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
by

(5.2)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle&=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle,\\
\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle&=-\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\kappa\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle,\end{aligned}\right.

for all
\boldsymbol{\chi},\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
, where

(5.3)

\displaystyle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big):=\frac{1}{2}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}+\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}.

Due to the presence of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}
term in (
5.2
), this is a two-step scheme and we need to prescribe
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}
and
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}
separately. This can be done, for instance, by solving with sufficiently small time step size,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle&=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle,\\
\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle&=-\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\kappa\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}\big),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle.\end{aligned}\right.

One could then show by arguments similar to those in this section that

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{1})}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{r}+k^{2}\big),

where
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
K_{0}
, and
|\mathscr{D}|
. This scheme is well-posed by similar argument as in Proposition
4.1
. The details are omitted for brevity. Subsequently, the proof in this section will focus on the general scheme (
5.2
) where
n\geq 1
, to avoid repetitive and lengthy arguments.

Next, we show some stability results analogous to Proposition
4.2
. The energy dissipation property is also satisfied unconditionally.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
be given and let
\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}\right)
be defined by
(
5.2
). Then for any
k>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1})\leq\mathcal{E}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}),

where
\mathcal{E}
was defined in (
1.5
). Moreover,

(5.6)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+k\lambda_{r}\sum_{m=0}^{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\lambda_{e}\sum_{m=0}^{n}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+|\mathscr{D}|\right),

where
C
depends only on
\kappa
and
\mu
.

Proof.

Firstly, taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}
in (
5.4
), then subtracting the results give

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\kappa}{4}\big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}\big)-\frac{\kappa\mu}{2}\big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\beta}{2}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+k\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}=0,

which implies (
5.5
) for
n=0
.

Similarly, for
n\geq 1
, setting
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
in (
5.2
) gives

(5.7)

\displaystyle\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

while setting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
gives

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2k}\big(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)+\frac{\kappa\mu}{2k}\big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)

(5.8)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\kappa}{4k}\big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}\big)-\frac{\beta}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{1}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Substituting (
5.8
) into (
5.7
) and rearranging yield the identity

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\kappa}{4}\big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}\big)-\frac{\kappa\mu}{2}\big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\beta}{2}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+k\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}=0,

which implies (
5.5
) and (
5.6
).
∎

We will also derive the stability of
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{2})
norm. The following identity will be used in the proof (where
\psi
was defined in (
5.3
)):

\displaystyle 2\big(\psi(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1})-\psi(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n})\big)

\displaystyle=\big(|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}|^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}|^{2}\big)\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)

(5.9)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}\big)\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}\big)\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
be given and let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
,
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
be defined by (
5.2
). Then for any
k>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=1}^{n}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C,

where
C
depends only on the coefficients of the equation,
K_{0}
, and
|\mathscr{D}|
.

Proof.

Adding the first equation in (
5.2
) at time steps
n+\frac{1}{2}
and
n-\frac{1}{2}
gives

\displaystyle\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

(5.11)

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{3}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle.

Subtracting the second equation in (
5.2
) at time step
n-\frac{1}{2}
from the same equation at time step
n+\frac{1}{2}
gives

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle

(5.12)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\left\langle\psi(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1})-\psi(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}})\big),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle.

Taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}
in (
5.11
) and
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{e}\big(\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)
in (
5.12
), then adding the resulting equations, and noting the identity

\displaystyle 2\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},

we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{4}{k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=(\lambda_{r}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e})\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{3}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\psi(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1})-\psi(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}),\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}})\big),\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=:S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}+S_{4}.

We will estimate each term on the right-hand side by applying Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities. Firstly,

(5.13)

\displaystyle|S_{1}|+|S_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq Ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+Ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the second term, similarly we have

\displaystyle|S_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\left(\left\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}+\left\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{3}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(5.14)

\displaystyle\leq Ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+Ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used (
5.6
) and the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{4}
. For the term in
S_{3}
, noting the identities (
5.9
) and (
5.12
), we have

\displaystyle|S_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

(5.15)

\displaystyle\leq Ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+Ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{k}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the final step we used (
5.6
), Young’s inequality, and the Sobolev embedding. Altogether, upon rearranging and summing the terms, (
5.13
), (
5.14
), and (
5.15
) imply

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{k}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+Ck\sum_{m=1}^{n}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\leq C,

where in the last step we used (
5.6
). This implies the required inequality.
∎

The above proposition implies the stability of
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})
norm under an additional assumption that the triangulation is quasi-uniform.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
be given and let
\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}\right)
be defined by
(
5.2
). Then for any
k>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(5.16)

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C.

Moreover, if the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is (globally) quasi-uniform, then

(5.17)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\leq C.

Here, the constant
C
depends only on the coefficients of the equation,
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
,
\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
, and
|\mathscr{D}|
.

Proof.

Taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
and applying Young’s and Hölder’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\kappa\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+4\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}.

Therefore, rearranging the terms, using the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{6}
(noting (
5.6
) and (
5.10
)), we inequality (
5.16
).
Inequality (
5.17
) then follows from (
2.18
), completing the proof of the proposition.
∎

Before proceeding to prove various estimates leading to the main theorem, we note the following inequalities. For
s=0
or
1
, by Taylor’s theorem,

(5.18)

\displaystyle\left\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}}\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\frac{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n-1}}{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}}+\left\|{\frac{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}{2}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{2},

and

(5.19)

\displaystyle\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}}\leq\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}}+\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{r+1-s}+k^{2},

where the assumptions on
\boldsymbol{u}
in (
2.2
) and (
2.8
) are used in the last step.

The following lemmas are needed to bound the nonlinear terms in the main theorem.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrary. The following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(5.20)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, if the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is quasi-uniform, then for any
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(5.21)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\right),\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2r}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}

(5.22)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Proof.

We write

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right).

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\right)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big).

The proof of (
5.20
) then follows by arguments similar to that in (
3.20
) (and noting (
5.18
)), without assuming
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is quasi-uniform.

Next, suppose the triangulation is quasi-uniform (and thus (
5.17
) holds in this case). By Hölder’s inequality and assumptions (
2.2
) on the exact solution, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left(\left\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{r+1}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+k^{2},

where in the last step we used (
5.17
), (
2.8
), and (
5.18
). Inequality (
5.21
) then follows by Young’s inequality. Finally, the proof of (
5.22
) is similar to that of (
4.31
).
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrary. The following inequality holds:

(5.23)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, if the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is quasi-uniform, then for any
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

(5.24)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

(5.25)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2r}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Proof.

Note that we have the identity

\displaystyle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}|^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}|^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}|^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}|^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}

(5.26)

\displaystyle\quad+\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\left(\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}+\left|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n}\big)}\right|^{2}}{2}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right|^{2}\right)=:S_{1}+S_{2}+\cdots+S_{7}.

We will proceed by bounding each term above. Terms containing
\boldsymbol{\theta}
will be bounded in the
\mathbb{L}^{4/3}
norm, while those containing
\boldsymbol{\rho}
will be bounded in the
\mathbb{L}^{6/5}
norm. For the first term, by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\left\|{S_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4/3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}.

Similarly for the next term,

\displaystyle\left\|{S_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6/5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

For the third and the fourth terms,

\displaystyle\left\|{S_{3}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4/3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}},

\displaystyle\left\|{S_{4}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6/5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\left\|{S_{5}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4/3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}},

\displaystyle\left\|{S_{6}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6/5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

For the last term, by Hölder’s inequality and Taylor’s theorem with integral remainder,

\displaystyle\left\|{S_{7}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{3/2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left(\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\left\|{\partial_{tt}\left|{\boldsymbol{u}(t)}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}

(5.27)

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\partial_{tt}\left|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}.

Altogether, using the assumptions on
\boldsymbol{u}
in (
2.2
) and inequality (
5.6
), by Hölder’s inequality we infer that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\big(\left\|{S_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4/3}}+\left\|{S_{3}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4/3}}+\left\|{S_{5}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4/3}}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}+\big(\left\|{S_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6/5}}+\left\|{S_{4}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6/5}}+\left\|{S_{6}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6/5}}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{S_{7}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}+\big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}

\displaystyle\quad+k^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4},

where in the last step we used Young’s inequality, (
2.15
), and (
2.8
). This proves (
5.23
).

Next, if the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is quasi-uniform, then (
5.17
) holds. As such, we can bound the
\mathbb{L}^{\infty}
norm of
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}
appearing in (
5.26
) (uniformly in
n
and
h
). Noting (
5.27
), we then obtain

(5.28)

\displaystyle\left\|{\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+k^{2}.

By Young’s inequality, we have for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim\left\|{\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

and thus inequality (
5.24
) follows from (
2.8
) and (
5.28
).

It remains to prove (
5.25
). Let
S:=S_{1}+S_{2}+\cdots+S_{7}
defined in (
5.26
). Then we have

\left\langle S,\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle=\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}S,\nabla\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle.

We will proceed by estimating
\left\|{\nabla S}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
using the expression (
5.26
) and the product rule for gradient. Firstly, by Hölder’s and Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla S_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}+\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}.

Similarly, for the second term,

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla S_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}\lesssim h^{r}.

The terms
\nabla S_{3}
up to
\nabla S_{6}
are estimated in a similar manner. The details are omitted for brevity. Lastly, the term
\nabla S_{7}
can be bounded as in (
5.27
), giving

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla S_{7}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\lesssim k^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{W}^{1,\infty}}\left\|{\partial_{tt}\left|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}.

Altogether, by Young’s inequality and stability of the projection operator, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle S,\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}S,\nabla\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\nabla S}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2r}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

thus proving (
5.25
).
∎

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrary. If the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is quasi-uniform, then for any
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\frac{\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\big)}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right),\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(5.29)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Proof.

After some tedious algebra, we can write the first component in the inner product on the left-hand side as

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle:=\frac{\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\big)}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}+\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}\right)\left(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)+\left(\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}+\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}}{2}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}\right)\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}\right)\right]\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}\right)+\left[\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\cdot\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{2}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)\right]\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{2}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle=:E_{1}+E_{2}+E_{3}+E_{4}+E_{5}.

We want to obtain a bound for
\left\|{E}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
. To this end, we will estimate the
\mathbb{L}^{2}
norm of each term above. Note that we have (
5.17
), which we will use without further mention. Firstly, by Hölder’s inequality,

(5.30)

\displaystyle\left\|{E_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
