\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\lesssim\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+h^{r+1}+k^{2},

where we used (
5.19
) and the triangle inequality. For the second term, note that by subtracting and adding
\frac{1}{2}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}+\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}\right)
, we have by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\left\|{\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}+\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}}{2}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}\right)\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}\right)\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}+\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}}{2}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+k^{2},

where in the last step we also used Taylor’s theorem with integral remainder as in (
5.27
). Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\|{E_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\frac{\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}}\right|^{2}+\left|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}}\right|^{2}}{2}-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}

(5.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+k^{2}.

The third term can be estimated in the same way as
E_{1}
, giving

(5.32)

\displaystyle\left\|{E_{3}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+h^{r+1}+k^{2},

while the terms
E_{4}
and
E_{5}
can be estimated in a similar manner as
E_{2}
, giving

(5.33)

\displaystyle\left\|{E_{4}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{E_{5}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+k^{2}.

Altogether, (
5.30
), (
5.31
), (
5.32
), (
5.33
), and Young’s inequality yield the required result.
∎

We have the following superconvergence estimates on
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}
and
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}
, which form an essential step in the proof of the main theorem, analogous to Proposition
3.8
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Assume that
\boldsymbol{u}
and
\boldsymbol{H}
satisfisfy (
2.2
). Then for
h,k>0
and
n\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor\}
,

(5.34)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}\big).

Moreover, if the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is globally quasi-uniform, then

(5.35)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=1}^{n-1}\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=1}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}\big),

(5.36)

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}\big),

where
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
|\mathscr{D}|
,
T
, and
K_{0}
(as defined in (
2.2
)), but is independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
.

Proof.

Subtracting (
2.1
) from (
5.2
) at time step
n+\frac{1}{2}
, using (
4.24
), (
4.25
) (and noting the definition of Ritz projection), we obtain for all
\boldsymbol{\chi}
,
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

(5.37)

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\chi}\right\rangle

and

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle-\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})\big),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle

(5.38)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle.

Taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
in (
5.37
) and
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{r}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
in (
5.38
), then adding the resulting expressions, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

(5.39)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle.

Next, taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{e}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

(5.40)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle.

Adding (
5.39
) and (
5.40
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})\big),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\big|\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle.

It remains to bound each term on the right-hand side. We apply (
5.19
) and Young’s inequality as necessary for the first five terms. The last four terms can be estimated by applying Lemma
5.4
and Lemma
5.5
. We then infer that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, rearranging the terms, and summing over
m\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\}
, we have

(5.41)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}\big)+Ck\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where
C
is a constant depending on the coefficients of the equation and
T
(but is independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
). The discrete Gronwall inequality then yields (
5.34
).

We aim to prove (
5.35
) next.
First, we consider the difference of the second equation in (
5.2
) at time steps
n+\frac{1}{2}
and
n-\frac{1}{2}
. After dividing the result by
k
, then subtracting it from the corresponding equation in (
2.1
) and taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{e}\big(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)
, we obtain (noting (
4.26
) and notations defined in (
5.1
)),

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left(\frac{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}}{k}\right)

\displaystyle=-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{H}(t_{n})-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{H}^{n},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})-\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\frac{\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\big)}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

(5.42)

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n})\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle.

By similar arguments, taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

(5.43)

\displaystyle\;+\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)+\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{3}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle.

Next, we add (
5.37
) at time step
n+\frac{1}{2}
and
n-\frac{1}{2}
. Taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}=\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)/k
, we have

\displaystyle 2\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)+\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle

(5.44)

\displaystyle\;-\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)+\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{3}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle.

Furthermore, we add (
5.38
) at time steps
n+\frac{1}{2}
and
n-\frac{1}{2}
. Taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{r}\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}
and rearranging the terms, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}\left(\frac{\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}}{k}\right)+\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n-\frac{1}{2}})\big),\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle

(5.45)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)+\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\big)-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle.

Adding (
5.42
), (
5.43
), (
5.44
), and (
5.45
), we then obtain, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left(\frac{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}}{k}\right)+\lambda_{r}\left(\frac{\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}}{k}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{H}(t_{n})-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{H}^{n},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})-\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\frac{\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\big)}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n})\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+2\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)+\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{3}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)+\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)+\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{3}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n-\frac{1}{2}})\big),\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)+\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\big)-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}\right\rangle

(5.46)

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}+\cdots+I_{16}.

There are sixteen terms involving inner products on the right-hand side of (
5.45
) which will be estimated in the following. Firstly, by Young’s inequality and (
2.8
),

(5.47)

\displaystyle\left|{I_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Secondly, by Young’s inequality and Taylor’s theorem (noting (
2.2
)),

\displaystyle\left|{I_{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{H}(t_{n})-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{H}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim k^{4}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
I_{3}
and
I_{6}
, by writing
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}+\boldsymbol{u}^{n}
then applying Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{3}}\right|+\left|{I_{6}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}.

The term
I_{4}
can be estimated in the same way as the term
I_{2}
(noting (
2.2
)), giving

\displaystyle\left|{I_{4}}\right|\lesssim k^{4}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

The term
I_{5}
can be estimated by using (
5.29
) with
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}
, giving

\displaystyle\left|{I_{5}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(5.48)

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used (
2.8
) and (
5.34
).
The terms
I_{7}
can be estimated directly by applying Young’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{7}}\right|\lesssim\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

The next two terms can be estimated using Young’s inequality and (
5.19
), yielding

\displaystyle\left|{I_{8}}\right|+\left|{I_{9}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Next, by Young’s inequality and (
2.8
),

\displaystyle\left|{I_{10}}\right|\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Now, the term
I_{12}
can be bounded in a similar way as the terms
I_{8}
and
I_{9}
to give

\displaystyle\left|{I_{12}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

while the terms
I_{11}
and
I_{13}
can be estimated by applying (
5.21
) and (
5.34
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{11}}\right|+\left|{I_{13}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

The next two terms are straightforward to estimate:

\displaystyle\left|{I_{14}}\right|+\left|{I_{15}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Finally, the last term can be estimated by using (
5.24
) to give

\displaystyle\left|{I_{16}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\epsilon\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, applying the above estimates for
I_{j}
, for
j=1,2,\ldots,16
, to (
5.46
), choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, and rearranging the terms yield

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left(\frac{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}}{k}\right)+\lambda_{r}\left(\frac{\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}}{k}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Summing over
m\in\{1,2,\ldots,n-1\}
, and noting the fact that
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{0}=R_{h}\boldsymbol{u}(0)
, so that

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4},

we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{e}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+k\sum_{m=1}^{n-1}\left(\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=1}^{n-1}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4},

where in the last step we also used (
5.34
). This implies inequality (
5.35
).

Finally, we will show (
5.36
). Taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
, rearranging the terms, and applying Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa\mu\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\left\langle\psi(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1})-\big|\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}})\big|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used (
2.8
), (
5.24
), and inequality (
5.35
) (which has been shown previously).
This, together with inequalities (
5.34
) and (
2.18
), yields (
5.36
). The proof is now complete.
∎

Proposition 5.8
.

Suppose that the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is globally quasi-uniform. Then for
h,k>0
and
n\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\big(h^{2r}+k^{4}\big),

where
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
|\mathscr{D}|
,
T
, and
K_{0}
, but is independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
.

Proof.

Successively taking
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{r}\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
in (
5.38
), then taking
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\lambda_{e}\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
in (
5.37
), and adding the resulting equations give

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\mu\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle+\gamma\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{r}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\kappa\mu\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\lambda_{e}\left\langle\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\left|{\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda_{e}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}})\big),\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=:J_{1}+J_{2}+\cdots+J_{11}.

We will estimate each term on the last line. For the first term, by Young’s inequality and (
5.19
), we have

\displaystyle\left|{J_{1}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

The term
J_{2}
will be left as is. For the third term, by Young’s inequality

\displaystyle\left|{J_{3}}\right|\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

The next two terms will be bounded by using (
5.21
) and (
5.24
) respectively, giving

\displaystyle\left|{J_{4}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{5}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

The term
J_{6}
can be estimated using Young’s inequality, giving

\displaystyle\left|{J_{6}}\right|\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Next, by using (
2.3
) and (
2.6
) (noting (
4.26
)), the term
J_{7}
can be written as

\displaystyle J_{7}=\lambda_{e}\left\langle\Pi_{h}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle=-\lambda_{e}\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle,

and similarly for the term
J_{9}
.
Therefore, by Young’s inequality and (
2.4
),

\displaystyle\left|{J_{7}}\right|+\left|{J_{9}}\right|\lesssim h^{2r}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Next, the terms
J_{8}
and
J_{11}
can be estimated by using (
2.6
) and Young’s inequality as

\displaystyle\left|{J_{8}}\right|+\left|{J_{11}}\right|\lesssim h^{2r}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Finally, the term
J_{10}
can be bounded by using (
5.25
) with
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
, giving

\displaystyle\left|{J_{9}}\right|\lesssim h^{2r}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2k}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{e}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2r}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

We can now proceed as in (
5.41
). Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, rearranging the terms, using (
5.35
), and summing over
m\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\big(h^{2r}+k^{4}\big)+Ck\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{m}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

The required estimate then follows from the discrete Gronwall inequality.
∎

Proposition 5.9
.

Suppose that the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}
is globally quasi-uniform. Then for
h,k>0
and
n\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\big(h^{2r}+k^{4}\big),

where
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
|\mathscr{D}|
,
T
, and
K_{0}
, but is independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
.

Proof.

Firstly, we follow the same argument leading to (
5.42
), but we now take
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
. Secondly, we follow the argument in (
5.43
), but with
\boldsymbol{\chi}=\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{h}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
. Adding the resulting equations and applying (
2.3
) and (
2.6
) as necessary, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}}{k}+\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2}\left\|{\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{e}}{2}\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n},\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle+\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{H}(t_{n})-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{H}^{n},\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\mu\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n},\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle+\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\left(\delta\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})-\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right),\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\left\langle\frac{\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1}\big)-\psi\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}\big)}{k}-\partial_{t}\left(\left|{\boldsymbol{u}^{n}}\right|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n}\right),\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\beta\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}\big(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot(\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{n})\big),\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\left(\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\right),\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\left(\delta\boldsymbol{\rho}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\delta\boldsymbol{u}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\right),\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\lambda_{r}}{2}\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right),\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)\right),\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{h}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}^{n-\frac{3}{2}}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\times\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\right),\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle

(5.49)

\displaystyle=:M_{1}+M_{2}+\cdots+M_{11}.

It remains to estimate each of the eleven terms involving inner products above. For
i=1,2,\ldots,6
, the terms
M_{i}
can be estimated in a similar way as the corresponding terms
I_{i}
in (
5.47
)–(
5.48
), giving

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{6}\left|{M_{i}}\right|\lesssim h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}+\left\|{\delta\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, by Young’s inequality, stability of the projection operator, and (
5.19
), we have

\displaystyle\left|{M_{7}}\right|+\left|{M_{8}}\right|+\left|{M_{9}}\right|\lesssim h^{2r}+k^{4}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Finally, by (
5.22
) (and noting Proposition
5.8
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left|{M_{10}}\right|+\left|{M_{11}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2r}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{n-2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2r}+k^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\Delta_{h}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\right)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Substituting these into (
5.49
), choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, and summing over
m\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\}
(noting Proposition
5.8
and inequality (
5.35
)), we obtain the required estimate.
∎

The following theorem on the rate of convergence of the numerical scheme (
5.2
) is now an immediate consequence of the previous propositions.

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{H})
be the solution of (
2.1
) which satisfies (
2.2
), and let
(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n})\in\mathbb{V}_{h}\times\mathbb{V}_{h}
be the solution of (
5.2
). Then for
h,k>0
and
n\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{m+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}\big),

\displaystyle k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{m}-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(t_{m})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2r}+k^{4}\big).

Moreover, if the triangulation is globally quasi-uniform, then for
h,k>0
and
n\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/k\rfloor\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}+k^{4}\big),

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n}-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\nabla\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2r}+k^{4}\big),

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}\big(t_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+k\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{m+\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{H}\big(t_{m+\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h^{2(r+1)}\left|{\ln h}\right|+k^{2}\big).

The constant
C
depends on the coefficients of the equation,
|\mathscr{D}|
,
T
, and
K_{0}
(as defined in (
2.2
)), but is independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
.

Proof.

Note that we have (
4.24
) and (
4.25
). The results then follow immediately by the estimates in Proposition
5.7
, Proposition
5.8
, Proposition
5.9
, inequalities (
2.8
), (
2.9
), and the triangle inequality.
∎

Remark 5.11
.

More careful estimates would allow us to obtain the stability of
\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{n}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{1})
(as in Proposition
4.5
) and to remove the global quasi-uniformity assumption for
d=1
and
2
(analogous to Proposition
4.11
and Theorem
4.12
). Further details are omitted for brevity.

6.
Approximation of the Regularised LLBloch Equation

In this section, we assume
\mu<0
. The aim here is to show that as
\lambda_{e}\to 0^{+}
, the solution of the LLBar equation converges to that of the LLBloch equation at a certain rate, thus we can treat the LLBar equation as a
regularised
LLBloch equation. We also outline some modifications needed in the schemes to ensure energy dissipativity at the discrete level for this regularised LLBloch equation (with
\lambda_{e}\Delta\boldsymbol{H}
as the regularisation term). Once these are done, we can then conclude that the numerical schemes proposed in Sections
3
,
4
, and
5
are also suitable to approximate the solution of the LLBloch equation (by choosing small
\lambda_{e}
and sufficiently small
h
and
k
).

First, we recall some known results about the LLBloch equation above
T_{\mathrm{c}}
(equation (
1.4
) with
\lambda_{e}=0
and
\mu<0
).
Given
T>0
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}\cap\mathbb{L}^{\infty}
, a global weak solution
\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{1})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{2})
exists
[
27
,
28
]
for
d=1,2,3
. This weak solution satisfies

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{2})}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}\leq K_{1}.

Furthermore, if
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{2}
, then a strong solution
\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{2})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{3})
exists, possibly only locally in time for
d=3
(cf.
[
28
]
). This strong solution satisfies

(6.1)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{2})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{3})}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}\leq K_{2},

where
K_{r}
, for
r=1,2
, are positive constants depending on the coefficients of the equation,
T
, and
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{r}}
.

For the LLBar equation with
\lambda_{e}=\varepsilon
and initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
, a weak solution
\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{1})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{3})
with a corresponding magnetic field
\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{1})
exist
[
36
]
. As implied by Proposition
3.1
, this weak solution enjoys the estimate

(6.2)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}\leq K_{3}.

where
K_{3}
depends on
T
and
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
, but is independent of
\varepsilon
. Furthermore, if
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{H}^{2}
, then we have a strong solution
\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{2})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{4})
.

We now show the convergence of the weak solution of the LLBar equation to that of the LLBloch equation as
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}
be a strong solution of the LLBar equation with
\lambda_{e}=\varepsilon
and initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{H}^{2}
. Let
\boldsymbol{u}
be a strong solution of the LLBloch equation (
\lambda_{e}=0
) with initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{2}
. Then

(6.3)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{H}^{2})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}-\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\varepsilon}.

Proof.

We write
\boldsymbol{v}:=\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}
and
\boldsymbol{B}:=\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}-\boldsymbol{H}
. Let
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}:=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
, so that
\boldsymbol{v}(0)=\boldsymbol{v}_{0}
. Since
\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}
and
\boldsymbol{u}
are strong solutions of the corresponding equations, we have

(6.4)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\boldsymbol{B}-\varepsilon\Delta\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}-\gamma\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}\times\boldsymbol{B}-\gamma\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{H},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{B}

\displaystyle=\Delta\boldsymbol{v}+\kappa\mu\boldsymbol{v}-\kappa|\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\boldsymbol{v}-\kappa\big((\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}+\boldsymbol{u})\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\big)\boldsymbol{u}-\beta\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}).

Successively taking the inner product of the first equation in (
6.4
) with
\boldsymbol{B}
, then taking the inner product of the second equation in (
6.4
) with
-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{B}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\varepsilon\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon},\nabla\boldsymbol{H}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{H},\boldsymbol{B}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\frac{\kappa\mu}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\boldsymbol{v},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle+\kappa\left\langle\big((\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}+\boldsymbol{u})\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\big)\boldsymbol{u},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle+\frac{\beta}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

where on the first equation we integrated by parts and used
\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon},\nabla\boldsymbol{B}\right\rangle=\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon},\nabla\boldsymbol{H}\right\rangle
.
Adding the above equations (noting
\mu<0
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\frac{\kappa\mu}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon},\nabla\boldsymbol{H}\right\rangle+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{H},\boldsymbol{B}\right\rangle-\kappa\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\boldsymbol{v},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle-\kappa\left\langle\big((\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}+\boldsymbol{u})\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\big)\boldsymbol{u},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle

(6.5)

\displaystyle=:J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}+J_{4}.

We will estimate each term on the last line. In the following, the constant
C
is independent of
\varepsilon
. Firstly, by Young’s inequality,

(6.6)

\displaystyle\left|{J_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+4\varepsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Next, by Young’s inequality and Sobolev embedding,

(6.7)

\displaystyle\left|{J_{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{r}}{8}\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\leq\frac{\lambda_{r}}{8}\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}.

We now aim to estimate
J_{3}
. To this end, substituting
\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}
by the first equation in (
6.4
) and integrating by parts as necessary, we have

(6.8)

\displaystyle J_{3}=-\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{B}\right\rangle+\kappa\varepsilon\left\langle\nabla\big(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\boldsymbol{v}\big),\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle+\kappa\gamma\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}\times\boldsymbol{B}\right\rangle=:J_{3a}+J_{3b}+J_{3c}.

For the terms
J_{3a}
and
J_{3b}
, by Young’s inequality and the Sobolev embedding (noting (
6.2
)) we have

\displaystyle\left|{J_{3a}}\right|\leq\kappa\lambda_{r}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{r}}{8}\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}.

For the terms
J_{3b}
and
J_{3c}
, similarly we obtain

\displaystyle\left|{J_{3b}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+4\varepsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}+4\varepsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{4}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{3c}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\gamma\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{r}}{8}\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}.

Substituting these estimates into (
6.8
), we obtain

(6.9)

\displaystyle\left|{J_{3}}\right|\leq\frac{\lambda_{r}}{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2},

where
C
is independent of
\varepsilon
.
The term
J_{4}
can be estimated in a similar manner as
J_{3}
, resulting in

(6.10)

\displaystyle\left|{J_{4}}\right|\leq\frac{\lambda_{r}}{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}.

Altogether, substituting the estimates (
6.6
), (
6.7
), (
6.9
), and (
6.10
) into (
6.5
), taking care to absorb relevant terms to the left-hand side, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}.

We now integrate both sides with respect to
t
. Note that by (
6.1
) and (
6.2
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}s\leq T+K_{2}+K_{3}.

Invoking the Gronwall inequality (noting (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) again), we obtain (
6.3
). This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Now that we have shown the convergence of strong solution of the LLBar equation to that of the LLBloch equation, the finite element schemes proposed in Section
4
and Section
5
(with small
\lambda_{e}
) would also be applicable to approximate the LLBloch equation with a small modification to ensure energy dissipativity (since now
\mu<0
), namely:

(1)

For the scheme (
4.1
), the term
\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle
is replaced by
\kappa\mu\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle
.

(2)

The scheme (
5.2
) can be kept as is.

In this case, for
\mu<0
, one could check that Proposition
4.1
, Proposition
4.2
, and Proposition
5.1
still hold with the same argument. We remark that the schemes remain unconditionally energy-stable even as
\lambda_{e}\to 0^{+}
as the constant
C
in (
4.6
) and (
5.6
) does not depend on
\lambda_{e}
. The rest of the results in Section
4
and
5
continue to hold almost verbatim, since the sign of
\mu
is not used in an essential way in the proofs.

7.
Numerical Simulations

Numerical simulations for the scheme (
4.1
) are performed using the open-source package
FEniCS

[
1
]
. Since the exact solution of the equation is not known, we use extrapolation to verify the spatial order of convergence experimentally. To this end, let
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(n)}
be the finite element solution with spatial step size
h
and time-step size
k=\lfloor T/n\rfloor
. For
s=0
or
1
, define the extrapolated order of convergence

\text{rate}_{s}:=\log_{2}\left[\frac{\max_{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}_{2h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}}}{\max_{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{e}_{h}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}}}\right],

where
\boldsymbol{e}_{h}(\boldsymbol{u}):=\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h/2}^{(n)}
and
\boldsymbol{e}_{h}(\boldsymbol{H}):=\boldsymbol{H}_{h}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{H}_{h/2}^{(n)}
. We expect that for scheme (
4.1
), when
k
is sufficiently small,
\text{rate}_{s}\approx h^{r+1-s}
. In these simulations, we take the domain
\mathscr{D}=[0,1]^{2}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
r=1
, i.e. piecewise linear polynomials.

7.1.
Simulation 1 (LLBar with
\mu>0
)

We take
k=2.5\times 10^{-3}
. The coefficients in (
1.4
) are taken to be
\lambda_{e}=1.0,\lambda_{r}=4.0,\gamma=10.0,\kappa=2.0,\mu=1.0
, and
\beta=-0.1
. The unit vector
\boldsymbol{e}=(0,0,1)^{\top}
. The initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
is given by

\boldsymbol{u}_{0}(x,y)=\big(\cos(2\pi x),\,\sin(2\pi y),\,2\cos(2\pi x)\sin(2\pi y)\big).

Snapshots of the magnetic spin field
\boldsymbol{u}
and the effective magnetic field
\boldsymbol{H}
at selected times are shown in Figure
1
and Figure
2
, respectively. The presence of a Bloch wall can be seen in the simulation around time
t=0.075
. Plots of
\boldsymbol{e}_{h}(\boldsymbol{u})
and
\boldsymbol{e}_{h}(\boldsymbol{H})
against
1/h
are shown in Figure
3(a)
and Figure
3(b)
.

7.3.
Simulation 3 (Energy dissipativity)

We take
k=2.5\times 10^{-3}
. The coefficients in (
1.4
) are taken to be
\lambda_{e}=0.001,\lambda_{r}=4.0,\gamma=5.0,\kappa=3.0
, and
\beta=0.2
. The unit vector
\boldsymbol{e}=(0,1,0)^{\top}
. The initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
is given by

\boldsymbol{u}_{0}(x,y)=\big(-2y\cos(2\pi x),\,4x^{2}\sin(2\pi y),\,2\cos(2\pi x)\sin(2\pi y)\big).

Recall that the energy of the system was defined in (
1.5
). Several plots of energy against time are shown in Figures
7(a)
,
7(b)
,
7(c)
, and
7(d)
for positive or negative
\mu
and various values of
h
and
k
. In all cases, the energy is seen to decrease monotonically at the discrete level.

(a)

Energy vs time for
\mu=20.0
,
k=0.0025

(b)

Energy vs time for
\mu=-5.0
,
k=0.0025

(c)

Energy vs time for
\mu=20.0
,
h=1/8

(d)

Energy vs time for
\mu=-1.0
,
h=1/8
